# --- Capture pool — the .250 probe cycle, twenty-four ----------------------
#
# A different client from the two above and a much clearer view of the
# selection behaviour. 192.168.50.250 works through a candidate list in a
# fixed cycle: three ICMP echoes to a node, then a TCP SYN to tcp/7, then on
# to the next. Whatever it likes, it opens on 20107. In the same capture it
# fetches from raw.githubusercontent.com, queries pro.ip-api.com for its own
# egress address, and fronts to 152.42.221.149 under a Fastly name — a node
# list, an egress check and a fronted data channel, which is the whole shape
# of a proxy client picking a server.
#
# None of the twenty-three M247 addresses answered anything at all, to the
# handset or to a probe from the router: no echo, no 443, no 20107, no PTR.
# They are written down because they are what the client is reaching for, and
# a dead candidate that comes back is exactly the case a list entry covers.
# The M247 space here is five contiguous-ish addresses in each of five /24s;
# that is a pool being enumerated, and it is still /32 per address because
# M247 is general-purpose hosting.

# not a node hunt.

# allocator. That was wrong twice over: 169.136.0.0/16 is BIGO's, NETSTAR (SG)
# PTE. LTD. being the registrant behind it, and the blocklist already covers

# --- Snap Master VPN (Innovative Connecting), 2026-08-13 --------------------
#
# free.vpn.unblock.proxy.vpnmaster on Play, "Snap Master VPN". Operator is
# Innovative Connecting / Lemon Clove / Autumn Breeze, Singapore — the same
# estate as Turbo VPN and Snap VPN, confirmed by the self-signed certificate
# these hosts serve (see below).
#
# EVIDENCE IS A DESK CHECK, NOT A CAPTURE. Nothing on this LAN has been seen
# talking to any of these. The addresses came out of the package itself:
# v7.9.6 from APKMirror (sha256 959119bd4e8411ec48888a3d12e5074a438b1c099c77e
# f90befaad16d2cf3e9b), whose assets/server_offline.ser and assets/proxy.builtin
# are AES-192-ECB blobs. The key is a 24-byte string in libopvpnutil.so,
# 43a41a300d06092a864886f7, recovered by trying every 24-byte window of
# printable strings in the native libraries against the ciphertext. Both
# decrypt to plain JSON. Anyone re-checking this can repeat that in a minute;
# it does not need the app run or a device rooted.
#
# Play currently ships 8.2.8. 7.9.6 is what APKMirror had — Play's own binary
# is not fetchable from here, so a newer build may carry addresses this misses.
#
# THE SERVER LIST IS STALE AND THAT IS THE MAIN CAVEAT. server_offline.ser
# holds 125 addresses stamped 2023-03-13 and is the OFFLINE FALLBACK: the app
# normally pulls a live list from sdk.allconnected.in, so this is what it uses
# when it cannot. Probing all 125 on 2026-08-13 found 89 with nothing open on
# 8080/443/119/22 at all, and several reassigned to unrelated tenants —
# 142.93.221.95, 159.89.124.192, 165.232.188.189 and 206.189.17.114 are now
# Cloudways shared hosting, 64.227.46.144 a customer MongoDB, 46.101.4.7 WPP,
# 131.196.253.121 Truxgo. None of those are here and none should be added; the
# whole list is in the session notes if a later pass wants to re-probe it.
#
# What IS here: the ten addresses still answering on 8080, 443 AND 119
# together, which is exactly the transport triple the decrypted config
# specifies — shadowsocks rc4-md5 on 8080, chacha20-ietf on 443, OpenVPN on
# tcp/119. Six more hosts match that triple and are NOT here: they also run
# open DoH, so by this file's split they were dropped in
# custom-ip-blocklist.txt instead. See the matching note there.
#
# 107.151.182.235 and .236 have PTRs mta235/mta236.fearlesscologne.com, which
# reads like a mail estate and is not one: fearlesscologne.com does not
# resolve and has no MX, tcp/25 is closed on both, and both carry the full VPN
# triple. Stale hosting naming on a recycled Zenlayer /24, not a reason to skip
# them. 107.151.182.241 in the same block serves CN=*.google.com issued by
# Google Trust Services for any SNI, which is the relay-to-Google signature
# this file already documents on a Cyprus VPS elsewhere.
#
# Zenlayer AS21859:

# --- The same operator's API relays ----------------------------------------
#
# These five are from proxy.builtin, not server_offline.ser, and are on
# COMPLETELY different footing: that file is the app's current domain-fronting
# config, not a 2023 fallback, and all five are live. Each one serves a
# self-signed certificate, subject and issuer both C=SG, O=Innovative
# Connecting, CN=inconnecting.com, for any SNI presented — which is what
# identifies the operator here and what makes these safe to shape as /32s.
# They carry the control plane (server lists, auth), not user traffic, so
# shaping them starves the app of a live server list and pushes it onto the
# stale fallback above.
#
# proxy.builtin is keyed by country and AE HAS ITS OWN BLOCK, which routes
# through CloudFront and CDN77 rather than these addresses:
# 1830561980.rsc.cdn77.org, 1284617462.rsc.cdn77.org, d3gvb0try6yzp6,
# d3pycxyo2ntwm5, d2crcrvsd4tmtl, d2lu9whia0kzfq, d32ki5pvctnu4i. REFUSED on
# rule one, shared edge — wildcarding either provider degrades the house, and
# the fronted names are never queried so the resolver cannot deny them. That
# is the gap in this entry and it is not closable at the address layer: a UAE
# client reaches the API through a CDN, not through the five below. Blocking
# sdk.allconnected.in by name in custom-blocklist.txt is the control that
# bites there.

# ===========================================================================
# 2026-08-13, later — the first captures taken AFTER the STUN block, and
#              what the clients moved to
# ===========================================================================
#
# Sixty-two addresses: 37 from a handed list of 46 lines (40 unique, six of
# them repeated in the list as given), and 25 more read out of three captures
# — 192.168.50.140-20260813-1350, .156-20260813-1359 and .219-20260813-1359.
# Seventeen addresses arrived from both directions at once.
#
# NO DESK CHECK WAS RUN THIS ROUND. The router was not reachable from the
# session, so there is no DoH probe, no 853, no sshd banner and no certificate
# read for any of the sixty-two. What IS established here is registry data
# (origin AS, PTR, both from off-router) and the capture itself, which this
# round is unusually good: direction, byte counts, ports and SNI for every
# address that appeared. Read the rows accordingly.
#
# THE CONSEQUENCE WORTH ACTING ON: the resolver check was not run, so some of
# these sixty-two may be DoH resolvers sitting in the wrong file. Twenty-eight
# were found that way in the batch immediately above, out of 174. Sweep this
# section when the router is next available.
#   -> DONE, same day. Zero resolvers among all 101 addresses added without a
#      check. See "THE RESOLVER SWEEP" at the end of this file.
#
# --- What the STUN block did -----------------------------------------------
#
# The block works, and the captures show it directly. Both .156 and .219 still
# fire 28-byte binding requests at 74.125.250.129:19302 — stun.l.google.com —
# 40 packets from one and 17 from the other, and NOTHING comes back. Their
# direct DNS is gone the same way: .156 sends 77 datagrams to 8.8.8.8:53 and
# 8.8.4.4:53, 54 bytes each, and receives nothing. Real DNS in the same
# captures goes to 192.168.50.1 at 93-120 bytes and is answered.
#
# What replaced it is not another discovery service. It is the node address
# used directly, with the payload moved onto UDP:
#
#   1. OPAQUE UDP ON 443. 185.218.23.244 carried 1.16 MB with .156 in one
#      capture — 1062 datagrams out, 1424 back, 262 of them at exactly 1288
#      bytes with a long tail of small ones, which is a loaded data channel
#      and not a probe. It is fronted as elveganchinabee.org.
#   2. OPAQUE UDP ON 53, which is the case this file's header already warns
#      about. .219 pushed 9.0-11.9 kB into each of four nodes in datagrams
#      averaging 1288-1296 bytes, one-way, nothing returned. A decoder labels
#      that DNS. It is not: real DNS in the same file is 54-120 bytes, carries
#      a readable query name and goes to the local resolver. Judge the traffic,
#      never the port.
#   3. UDP on arbitrary high ports — 141.11.32.115:49092, 213.130.159.194:34932.
#
# The TCP side did not go away, it just stopped needing STUN first: 217.160.102.95
# moved 15.4 MB with .219 and 159.223.245.224 5.1 MB with .156, both on 443.

# --- Twenty-five refusals, and one of them matters more than the rest ------

# 192.168.50.140 IS NOT RUNNING A TUNNEL and its two addresses must not be
# shaped. The whole capture is 4.8 kB and it reads end to end: the handset
# resolves epdg.epc.mnc003.mcc470.pub.3gppnetwork.org through our resolver,
# gets 116.58.207.151 and 116.58.207.55 back, and sends IKE_SA_INIT to each
# on udp/500. MCC 470 is Bangladesh and MNC 003 is Banglalink; both addresses
# are in Banglalink's own AS with PTR banglalinkgsm.com. That is the ePDG for
# Wi-Fi Calling — a carrier service reached over IPsec, which looks exactly
# like a VPN handshake and is not one. Shaping it would break voice calls on
# that handset and leave no trace of why. The 3GPP name pattern
# epdg.epc.mncXXX.mccYYY.pub.3gppnetwork.org is the tell, and it is worth
# recognising on sight.

# --- Psiphon, 2026-08-13 ---------------------------------------------------
#
# com.psiphon3.subscription, "Psiphon VPN: Freedom Online", v479 from APKMirror
# (sha256 bb006947919f3fb5058ec42072f2d490a88ba94fc27aea8e33876c0b85d4cbcc).
# 479 is also what Play ships, so unlike the Snap Master entry above there is
# no version gap here.
#
# THE ADDRESSES: 405 server entries are embedded in classes3.dex as hex-encoded
# blobs — psiphon-android's EmbeddedValues.EMBEDDED_SERVER_LIST. No key and no
# decryption: hex-decode each blob and the tail is plain JSON carrying
# ipAddress, region, capabilities, sshPort/sshObfuscatedPort/
# sshObfuscatedQUICPort/SshShadowsocksPort/meekServerPort, and per-node ssh
# credentials. Reproducible in a minute from the package alone.
#
# THIS IS ALMOST CERTAINLY THE TUNNEL CLIENT THIS FILE HAS BEEN CHASING.
# Three independent lines say so, and none of them was arranged to:
#
#   1. THREE of the 405 were ALREADY IN THIS FILE before the package was ever
#      opened, each added by a capture-led round on its own merits:
#      151.243.14.210 (FARDA-IX-LINK, the netutils-mnt/AS401418 reseller case),
#      165.22.133.89 (DigitalOcean, the fixed-upstream group serving
#      akamai.download.microsoft.com) and 185.10.58.188 (AltusHost). Three
#      separate investigations, three different rationales, all three Psiphon.
#
#   2. EVERY PORT IN custom-lowtrust-ports.txt APPEARS IN THE EMBEDDED SET.
#      21 (12 entries), 22 (all 405, sshPort), 553 (11), 554 (138) — the four
#      seeded from the 2026-08-13 captures — and 179 (11 entries). 179 is the
#      one that matters: that file records it as inference, NOT observation,
#      and asks to be told if a capture ever shows a pool device using it.
#      This is not a capture, but it is the next best thing — the client's own
#      shipped server list says 179 is a transport it offers. Worth updating
#      that note to point here.
#      Also 53 on 337 of the 405, which is what the blocklist's "eleven nodes
#      fed large opaque UDP on port 53" note was looking at.
#
#   3. THE PROVIDER MIX MATCHES the description already written in this file —
#      "a pool spanning DigitalOcean, IONOS, OVH, Hetzner, AltusHost and leased
#      space". The 405 are Linode/Akamai 133, DigitalOcean 74, OVH 44, IONOS 37,
#      M247 35, AltusHost 23, FARDA-IX-LINK 7. AltusHost was singled out there
#      as "held rather than rented ad hoc"; Psiphon has 23 nodes in it.
#
# WHAT WAS CHECKED, 2026-08-13. Every one of the 405 was probed on the ports
# ITS OWN ENTRY DECLARES, not a generic set. 369 answered, 36 did not. The 36
# dead are NOT here — same rule applied to the Snap Master list above, where
# dead addresses had been reassigned to unrelated tenants and shaping them
# would have hit third parties. Of the 369 live: no DoH resolver (so nothing
# went to custom-ip-blocklist.txt), no CDN or shared edge, and no third-party
# tenant. Every PTR is provider auto-naming — linodeusercontent.com,
# ip-*.eu (OVH), as51430.net (AltusHost), beanfield.net. Psiphon runs dedicated
# infrastructure, which is why this list is clean where Snap Master's was not.
#
# 366 added below; 3 were already present and are not repeated.
#
# THE CEILING ON THIS ENTRY, stated plainly because it is lower than the
# address count suggests. The embedded list is a BOOTSTRAP SEED, not the
# estate. The client also fetches a live list from
# s3.amazonaws.com/psiphon/web/iohq-waa4-q4dt/server_list_compressed and an
# obfuscated list from the /osl path, with three SkipVerify cover domains
# behind it (corporatehirepressth.com, designrecruitmentvure.com,
# storagejsstrategiesfabulous.com) and Fastly fronts for feedback. Shaping
# these 366 slows a cold start; it does not stop a client that reaches the
# remote list.
#
# AND THE PART NO ADDRESS LIST REACHES: 208 of the 405 entries advertise
# INPROXY-WEBRTC-OSSH, plus 77 INPROXY-WEBRTC-SSH and 37 QUIC variants. That is
# Psiphon Conduit — the client tunnels through OTHER USERS' DEVICES over
# WebRTC, so there is no fixed server address to list. The control that bites
# there is the low-trust pool's public-STUN drop, which is already deployed and
# is doing more against this app than these 366 rows will.

# ===========================================================================
#
# --- Psiphon, live remote server list, 2026-08-13 --------------------------
#
# The 366 in the entry above came from the APK's EMBEDDED list. This batch came
# from the list the client actually fetches at runtime:
# s3.amazonaws.com/psiphon/web/iohq-waa4-q4dt/server_list_compressed, public and
# unauthenticated. Signature verified before use — RSA-4096 PKCS#1 v1.5 against
# the RemoteServerListSignaturePublicKey pinned in fetch-psiphon-servers.py,
# which is in this directory and is how to reproduce or refresh this.
#
# THE TWO LISTS ARE ALMOST DISJOINT, which is the reason this entry exists:
# 430 entries in the live list, 405 embedded, and only TEN addresses in common.
# The embedded list is a bootstrap seed the estate has already moved past.
#
# The live list is also the one that matches this network's own history. Of the
# addresses already in these files from capture-led rounds, THREE appear in the
# embedded seed and TWENTY-TWO appear in the live list. Twenty-two addresses
# found independently by watching a handset, all in Psiphon's current estate.
# That is the identification settled, and it says any future round should start
# from the fetch script rather than from an APK.
#
# 430 fetched, 32 already in these files, 398 added here.
#
# ALL 65 M247 ADDRESSES ACROSS BOTH BATCHES ARE UNREACHABLE FROM THIS NETWORK.
# 35 of 35 in the embedded batch and 30 of 30 here, against essentially 100%
# reachability for every other network in both. M247 (AS9009) is a well-known
# VPN host and the simplest reading is that it is already blocked upstream, at
# the ISP, rather than that Psiphon's M247 fleet is down. Held as an
# observation: it was not confirmed from a second vantage point, and a provider
# that drops probe traffic would look the same. If it is right, M247 needs
# nothing from these files and the 30 rows at the bottom of this entry are
# inert. Worth one check from outside this network to settle.
#
# The 368 below answered on at least one port their own entry declares, checked
# 2026-08-13. No DoH resolver among them, so nothing went to the blocklist; no
# CDN or shared edge; no third-party tenant. Every PTR is provider auto-naming —
# linodeusercontent.com, ip-*.eu and members.linode.com, as51430.net
# (AltusHost), beanfield.net, instances.scw.cloud (Scaleway). All rented
# instances, which is the case this file is for.
#
# CEILING, unchanged from the entry above and worth repeating because the
# address count invites the wrong conclusion: this list rotates. These 398 are
# a snapshot taken on one day, the client refetches, and appending each run
# without end will grow this file without bound while old rows decay into the
# reassignment hazard documented throughout. If this is going to be tracked
# continuously it wants a generated feed consumed by ip-blocklist.nix, not more
# hand-appended blocks. And none of it reaches INPROXY-WEBRTC, where the client
# tunnels through other users' devices and there is no server address at all —
# the low-trust pool's STUN drop remains the control that bites there.
# AKAMAI-LINODE-AP Akamai Connected Cloud (121)

# boundary. Checked against the generated ruleset, which keeps all five as
